Figure 12.
SOGAT definition of AdapTT

Figure

12
presents a definition of AdapTT using a second-order generalized algebraic theory (SOGAT)
Uemura, 2021
.
In this presentation, only the part of the context that is required to specify a judgment is explicit: informally, all the sorts, operations and equations can be understood as parametrized by an implicit additional context.
The presentation of
Fig.

2
is derived from this presentation by the
translation of a SOGAT into a generalized algebraic theory (GAT)
Kaposi and Xie, 2024
.

The presentation of a bare category with families, which we extend, features
a sort
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Ty}}
classifying well-formed types, and a dependent sort

\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Tm}}\,A
classify well-formed terms of type
A\mathrel{:}\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Ty}}
.
The sort
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Tm}}
of terms is flagged as a representable sort:
the construction of the corresponding (first-order) generalized algebraic theory uses this information to construct explicit contexts populated with variables for representable sorts, here only
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Tm}}
. This is enough to derive all structural rules of a CwF, including
Rules
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
,
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
,

2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
and their equations.

To this basic structure, we add
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Ad}}\,A\,B
, the sort
of adapters between the types
A
and
B
.
The operation
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.58,0,0.83}id}}
provides the identity adapter, while
{\color[rgb]{0.58,0,0.83}\circ}
composes two adapters with compatible target and source.
Together with the equations
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.132,0.545,0.132}idl}}
,
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.132,0.545,0.132}idr}}
and
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.132,0.545,0.132}assoc}}
, these operations equip
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Ty}}
with the structure of a category.
Again, because this is a SOGAT, all these sorts operations are automatically stable under
substitution in the derived GAT,
i.e.
Rules
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
,
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
,
and
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
.

Adapters
a:\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Ad}}\,A\,B
act on terms
t:\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Tm}}A
to obtain another term
t{\color[rgb]{0.58,0,0.83}\langle}a{\color[rgb]{0.58,0,0.83}\rangle}:\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.255,0.41,0.884}Tm}}B
.
This action must respect identity adapters (
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.132,0.545,0.132}adapt/id}}
) and composition of adapters (
\operatorname{{\color[rgb]{0.132,0.545,0.132}adapt/\circ}}
), hence satisfying functoriality laws that make
\Ty
a
(
\cSet
-valued) functor on the category of types and adapters, thus an internal category
to presheaves on
\Ctx
after the translation.

Appendix B
Correspondence between gCwFs and NatMod
DO
s

In this section, we show the correspondence between split gCwFs and NatMod
DO
s,
corresponding to
Section

2.3
and in particular
Theorem

2.13
.

B.1.
Categorical refresher

2-categorical Yoneda

We start by recalling the categorical material we need. First, recall the (2-categorical) Yoneda lemma
Kelly, 1982
: given
A
in
\Pst(C)
, for every object
c
of
C
, there is an isomorphism of categories
A(c)\simeq\yon c\to A
between
A(c)
and the category of functors
\yon c\to A
.

Discrete opfibrations

To assess the task at hand, we expand on
Definition

2.2
, in particular regarding the condition that
p
is a discrete opfibration. We recall that a functor
F\mathrel{:}C\to D
is a discrete opfibration if for every object
a
in
C
and any morphism
f^{\prime}\mathrel{:}F(a)\to b^{\prime}
in
D
, there exists a unique morphism
f\mathrel{:}a\to b
in
C
such that
F(f)=f^{\prime}
, called the lift of
f^{\prime}
at
a
. Given any
2
-category
C
, we say that a morphism
f\mathrel{:}a\to b
in
C
is a discrete opfibration if for every
c
in
C
, the functor
C(c,f)\mathrel{:}C(c,a)\to C(c,b)
is a discrete opfibration of categories, and for any
g\mathrel{:}c\to d
, the following diagram is a morphism of discrete opfibrations,

i.e.
the lift of the image under the lower horizontal map is the image of the lift under the upper horizontal map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(d,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(c,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(d,b)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(c,b)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(g,a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(d,f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(c,f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(g,b)}

On the morphism
p\mathrel{:}\Tm\to\Ty
in the
2
-category
\Pst(\Ctx)
, the first part of this definition when evaluated on a representable presheaf
\yon\Gamma
gives that
p_{\Gamma}
is a discrete opfibration, which entails the existence of the type casting operation
t\langle a\rangle
and satisfaction of Rules
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
and
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
. The second condition, when evaluated on
\yon\sigma
for some
\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\to\Delta
in
\Ctx
gives exactly the validation of Rule
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
.

Grothendieck construction

We also make extensive use of the Grothendieck constructions and its properties, as well as the special case of the category of elements, and their link with fibrations and discrete fibrations. Given a functor
F\mathrel{:}C^{\op}\to\Cat
, we define the Grothendieck construction
\int F
as the category whose objects are pairs
(c,x)
where
c
is an object of
C
and
x
is an object of
F(c)
, and whose morphisms
(c,x)\to(d,y)
are pairs
(f,\phi)
where
f\mathrel{:}c\to d
is a morphism in
C
and
\phi\mathrel{:}x\to F(f)(y)
is a morphism in
F(c)
. The first projection
\int F\to C
induces a split fibration. In fact, the Grothendieck construction induces an equivalence of
2
-categories between the
2
-category of functors
C^{\op}\to\Cat
and the
2
-category of split fibrations over
C

Jacobs, 2001
. The reciprocal functor is obtained by taking a split fibration
F
over
C
to the functor
F^{-1}\mathrel{:}C^{\op}\to\Cat
sending every object to its fiber, the splitness axiom giving the functorial action.

B.2.
Proofs for
Section

2.3

We consider a split gCwF given by the following data

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{U}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\top}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

and we denote respectively
\eta\mathrel{:}\symup{id}\Rightarrow\Delta\Sigma
and
\epsilon\mathrel{:}\Sigma\Delta\Rightarrow\symup{id}
the unit and the counit of the adjunction. Given a morphism
\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
in the category
\mathcal{B}
, and objects
t
and
A
in the fiber over
\Gamma^{\prime}
of
u
and
u^{\prime}
respectively, we denote respectively
\bar{\sigma}\mathrel{:}t[\sigma]\to t
and
\bar{\sigma}\mathrel{:}A[\sigma]\to A
the chosen cartesian lifts of
\sigma
at
t
and
A
. Since
\dot{u}
and
u
are split this action respects composition and identities.

In order to provide the type cast operation of
Proposition

2.12
, it will be convenient to consider a particular family of morphism in the split gCwF. Given an object
t
of
\dot{\mathcal{U}}
and a vertical morphism
a:\Sigma(t)\to B
in
\mathcal{U}
, we define

\tau_{t,a}=\dot{u}(\Delta(a)\circ\eta_{t})

The special role of these morphisms can be understood in the light of
Theorem

2.13
, where under the stated equivalence,
t\mathrel{:}\Tm(\Gamma)
is a term of type
A\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma)
and
a\mathrel{:}A\to B
is an adapter. Then the morphism
\tau_{t,a}
corresponds to the substitution
\symup{id}_{\Gamma}\mathbin{\triangleright}t\langle a\rangle\mathrel{:}\Gamma\to\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}B
. We thus use these morphisms, which are easy to construct in the gCwF formalism, in order to recover the type cast operations. We first show the following technical result

Lemma B.1.

With the same notations as above, the following equation holds

\epsilon_{B}\circ\Sigma(\overline{\tau_{t,a}})=\symup{id}_{B}

Proof.

First note that using the naturality of
\epsilon
, the triangle equation
for adjunctions and the verticality of
a
we have:

\displaystyle u(\epsilon_{B}\circ\Sigma(\overline{\tau_{t,a}}))

\displaystyle=u(\epsilon_{B})\circ\tau_{t,a}

\displaystyle=u(\epsilon_{B})\circ u(\Sigma(\Delta(a)\circ\eta_{t}))

\displaystyle=u(\epsilon_{B}\circ\Sigma\Delta(a)\circ\Sigma\eta_{t})

\displaystyle=u(a\circ\epsilon_{\Sigma(t)}\circ\Sigma\eta_{t})

\displaystyle=u(a)

\displaystyle=\symup{id}_{u(B)}

Moreover, we note that the maps
\epsilon_{B}
and
\overline{\tau_{t,a}}
are cartesian, and
\Sigma
preserves cartesian maps, hence
\epsilon_{B}\circ\Sigma(\overline{\tau_{t,a}})
is cartesian. Being a cartesian map whose image is the identity, it is itself an identity by splitness of the fibration
u
.
∎

We now prove a more precise version of
Proposition

2.12
.

Proposition B.2.

in a split gCwF, the functor
\Sigma
induces a discrete opfibration of
\Cat
-valued presheaves from
\dot{u}^{-1}
to
u^{-1}
. Explicitly, considering an object
t
of
\dot{\mathcal{U}}
and a vertical morphism
a:\Sigma(t)\to B
in
\mathcal{U}
, there exists a unique object
t\langle a\rangle
in
\dot{\mathcal{U}}
equipped with a vertical morphism
\tilde{a}:t\to t\langle a\rangle
such that

\displaystyle\Sigma(t\langle a\rangle)

\displaystyle=B

\displaystyle\Sigma(\tilde{a})

\displaystyle=a

This operation moreover satisfies the equation
t\langle a\rangle[\sigma]=t[\sigma]\langle a[\sigma]\rangle
.

Proof of
Proposition

B.2
.

Considering an object
t
of
\dot{\mathcal{U}}
and a vertical morphism
a:\Sigma(t)\to B
in
\mathcal{U}
, we define
t\langle a\rangle=\Delta(B)[\tau_{t,a}]
with the vertical morphism
\tilde{a}:t\to t\langle a\rangle
given by the lifting property justified by the equation
\dot{u}(t\langle a\rangle)=\dot{u}(t)
, depicted below in the category
\dot{\mathcal{U}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\langle a\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\tau_{t,a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta(a)\circ\eta_{t}}

It suffices to show that these satisfy the required equations. For the first equation, we note that since
\epsilon_{B}
is cartesian, we have
\Sigma\Delta(B)=B[u(\epsilon_{B})]
. Thus, using
Lemma

B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma(t\langle a\rangle)

\displaystyle=\Sigma\Delta(B)[\Sigma\overline{\tau_{t,a}}]=B[\epsilon_{B}\circ\Sigma\overline{\tau_{t,a}}]=B

The second equation is given by the following commutative diagram, where the left square commutes by definition of
\tilde{a}
, the right square commutes by naturality, the top region commutes by the triangle equation, and the lower region commutes by
Lemma

B.1
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma t}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma\Delta\Sigma t}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma t}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B[u(\epsilon_{B})]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma(\eta_{t})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma\tilde{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\Sigma t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma\Delta a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma(\bar{\tau_{a}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{B}}

We next prove uniqueness. Consider an object
t^{\prime}
and a vertical morphism
b\mathrel{:}t\to t^{\prime}
such that
\Sigma t^{\prime}=B
and
\Sigma b=a
, we show that necessarily
b=\tilde{a}
. For this we consider the following naturality square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\Sigma t}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\Sigma t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\Sigma b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{t^{\prime}}}

Taking the image by
\dot{u}
of this naturality square yields by
verticality of the morphism
b
,

\displaystyle\dot{u}(\eta_{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\dot{u}(\tau_{a})

Since
\eta_{t^{\prime}}
is cartesian it is its own chosen lift by assumption, we conclude that
\eta_{t^{\prime}}=\overline{\tau_{t,a}}
, which proves that
t^{\prime}=t\langle a\rangle
. Moreover, the morphism
b
is a vertical morphism making the following diagram commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\langle a\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\tau_{t,a}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{t,a}}

By uniqueness of such a morphism, we conclude that
b=\tilde{a}
.

Finally, we consider a morphism
\sigma:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
and show the equation
t\langle a\rangle[\sigma]=t[\sigma]\langle a[\sigma]\rangle
. This is given by the following computation, where the second line is obtained by the fact that
\Delta
preserves chosen lifts, the third line is given by definition of
a[\sigma]
, and the fourth line is given by naturality of
\eta
and by the fact that
\Sigma
preserves chosen lifts:

\displaystyle t[\sigma]\langle a[\sigma]\rangle

\displaystyle=\Delta(B[\sigma])[\tau_{t[\sigma],a[\sigma]}]

\displaystyle=\Delta B[\Delta\sigma][\Delta(a[\sigma])\circ\eta_{t[\sigma]}]

\displaystyle=\Delta B[\Delta(a)\circ\Delta(\sigma)\circ\eta_{t[\sigma]}]

\displaystyle=\Delta B[\tau_{t,a}][\sigma]

∎

\displaystyle=t\langle a\rangle[\sigma]

Theorem B.3.

The category of split gCwFs and the category of NatMod
DO
s are equivalent.

Proof.

Starting with a split gCwF, we define a NatMod
DO
by considering the presheaves of
\Ty
and
\Tm
to be given respectively by
u^{-1}
and
\dot{u}^{-1}
. The morphism of fibrations
\Sigma
then induces a natural transformation
p\mathrel{:}\Tm\overset{\mathchar 58\relax}{\to}{}\Ty
. By
Proposition

B.2
,
p
is a discrete opfibration. Moreover, the existence of
\Delta
implies that
p
is representable. Indeed, given an object
A
in
\Ty(\Gamma)
, we define
\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A=\dot{u}(\Delta A)
, together with
\mathord{\uparrow}=\dot{u}(\epsilon_{A})
and
\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}
to be the element of
\dot{\mathcal{U}}
corresponding to
\Delta(A)
. Since
\epsilon_{A}
is its own lift,
\Sigma(\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}})=A[\mathord{\uparrow}]
and so
\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}
is an object of
\Tm(\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,A[\mathord{\uparrow}])
. Given a morphism
\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{B}
, and objects
A\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma^{\prime})
and
t\mathrel{:}\Tm(\Gamma,A[\sigma])
, we define the morphism
\sigma\mathbin{\triangleright}t=\dot{u}(\Delta(\bar{\sigma})\circ\eta_{t})
where
\bar{\sigma}\mathrel{:}A[\sigma]\to A
is the cartesian lift of
\sigma
at
A
through
u
. In order to check that these satisfy the adequate universal property, we verify the three following conditions:

(1)

Given
\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
with
A\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma^{\prime})
and
t\mathrel{:}\Tm(\Gamma,A[\sigma])
, we have
\mathord{\uparrow}\circ(\sigma\triangleright t)=\sigma
. This is proven by the following computation using the naturality of
\epsilon
and one triangle identity for adjunctions

\displaystyle\mathord{\uparrow}\circ(\sigma\triangleright t)

\displaystyle=u(\epsilon_{\Sigma\Delta\Sigma t})\circ\dot{u}(\Delta(\bar{\sigma})\circ\eta_{t})

\displaystyle=u(\epsilon_{\Sigma\Delta\Sigma t}\circ\Sigma\Delta(\bar{\sigma})\circ\Sigma\eta_{t})

\displaystyle=u(\bar{\sigma}\circ\epsilon_{\Sigma t}\circ\Sigma\eta_{t})

\displaystyle=u(\bar{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\sigma

(2)

Given
\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
with
A\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma^{\prime})
and
t\mathrel{:}\Tm(\Gamma,A[\sigma])
, we have
\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}[\sigma\mathbin{\triangleright}t]=t
. This is given by the fact that since
\Delta
preserves chosen lifts and
\eta_{t}
is the chosen lift of its projection, by splitness of
\dot{u}
, the following morphism is the chosen lift of its projection

\Delta(\bar{\sigma})\circ\eta_{t}\mathrel{:}t\to\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}

(3)

For
\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}\mathbin{\triangleright}A
, we have
\sigma=(\mathord{\uparrow}\circ\sigma)\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}[\sigma]
. To show this, we denote
\bar{\sigma}\mathrel{:}\Delta(A)[\sigma]\to\Delta(A)
the cartesian lift of
\sigma
at
\Delta(A)
. Using the fact that
\epsilon_{A}
is the chosen lift of its projection, as well as the naturality of
\eta
and the other triangle identity for adjunction, we obtained the desired identity by the following computation:

\displaystyle(\mathord{\uparrow}\circ\sigma)\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\dot{u}(\Delta(\epsilon_{A}\circ\Sigma\bar{\sigma})\circ\eta_{\Delta(A)[\sigma]})

\displaystyle=\dot{u}(\Delta\epsilon_{A}\circ\eta_{\Delta(A)}\circ\bar{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\dot{u}(\bar{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\bar{\sigma}

Conversely, starting with a NatMod
DO

\Ctx
, we define a split gCwF by letting
\mathcal{B}
be
\Ctx
, the split fibration
u\mathrel{:}\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{B}
to be the Grothendieck construction of the functor
\Ty\mathrel{:}\Ctx\to\Cat
and the split fibration
\dot{u}\mathrel{:}\dot{\mathcal{U}}\to\Cat
to be the Grothendieck construction of the functor
\Tm\mathrel{:}\Ctx\to\Cat
. The natural transformation
p\mathrel{:}\Tm\overset{\mathchar 58\relax}{\to}{}\Ty
then induces a morphism of split fibrations
\Sigma
, which fits into the following picture:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int\Tm}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int\Ty}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

where both
u
and
\dot{u}
are split opfibrations and
\Sigma
is a morphism of fibration that preserves chosen lifts. To complete the gCwF structure, it suffices to check that
\int_{\Gamma}\tyop_{\Gamma}
has a right adjoint that preserves chosen lifts, with unit and counit being componentwise the chosen lift of their projection. We explicitly define the right adjoint

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int\Ty}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int\Tm}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma,A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma,A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Delta,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Delta\mathbin{\triangleright}B,\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma,a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\sigma\circ\mathord{\uparrow})\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}\langle a[\mathord{\uparrow}]\rangle{,}a[\mathord{\uparrow}])}

Note that
\Delta
sends
(\sigma,\symup{id})
onto a pair whose second component is an identity, and hence it preserves chosen lifts. Moreover, we have natural transformations

\displaystyle\eta_{\Gamma,t}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}(\Gamma,t)\to(\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}p_{\Gamma}(t),\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}})

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\Gamma,A}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}(\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,A[\mathord{\uparrow}])\to(\Gamma,A)

\displaystyle\eta_{\Gamma,t}

\displaystyle=(\symup{id}\mathbin{\triangleright}t,\symup{id})

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\Gamma,A}

\displaystyle=(\mathord{\uparrow},\symup{id})

Since the second component is always the identity, both these natural transformations are componentwise the chosen lift of their projection. In order to check the triangle identities it thus suffices to check their that they hold on the first components. The first triangle identity is given, for
t\mathrel{:}\Tm(\Gamma)
by the equation

\mathord{\uparrow}_{\Gamma,p_{\Gamma}(t)}\circ(\symup{id}_{\Gamma}\mathbin{\triangleright}t)=\symup{id}_{\Gamma}

The second equation is given, for
A\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma)
, by the following computation:

\displaystyle((\mathord{\uparrow}_{\Gamma,A}\circ\mathord{\uparrow}_{\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,A[\mathord{\uparrow}]})

\displaystyle\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}_{\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,A[\mathord{\uparrow}]})\circ(\symup{id}_{\Gamma,A}\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}_{\Gamma,A})

\displaystyle=(\mathord{\uparrow}_{\Gamma,A}\circ\mathord{\uparrow}_{\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,A[\mathord{\uparrow}]}\circ(\symup{id}_{\Gamma,A}\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}_{\Gamma,A}))\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}_{\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A,A[\mathord{\uparrow}]}[\symup{id}_{\Gamma,A}\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}_{\Gamma,A}]

\displaystyle=\mathord{\uparrow}_{\Gamma,A}\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{tm}}_{\Gamma,A}

\displaystyle=\symup{id}_{\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}A}

These two functors define an equivalence of
2
-categories, induced by the equivalence between split fibrations over
\Ctx
and functors
\Ctx^{\op}\to\Cat
defined by the Grothendieck construction and the fiber.
∎

Appendix C
Alternative presentation of AdapTT

In this section, we present an alternate description of the theory
AdapTT, that was suggested by an anonymous reviewer. This version is a
bit more verbose but gives insight regarding the required equality on term
constructors. We present here the rules together with a description of their
intended semantics, keeping in mind that it should have the same models as
AdapTT, namely, NatMod
DO
. This presentation is obtained by removing all the
equations that we impose on terms, and instead introducing an additional
judgment:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\quad\Gamma\vdash b\mathrel{:}B\quad\Gamma\vdash f\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\quad}{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}

The reader may think of this judgment as denoting

\Gamma\vdash a\langle f\rangle\equiv b\mathrel{:}B
, as this is what we will force it to be.
We write this judgment with explicit witnesses, even thought
the discreteness condition we impose for the opfibration enforces that
there is (definitionally) at most one inhabitant. Semantically, in the NatMod
DO
structure,
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}

describes morphisms
e
in
\Tm(\Gamma)
whose image
by
p
is the morphism
f
in the category
\Ty(\Gamma)
.

C.1.
Category structure

The following rules axiomatise the category structure of

\Tm(\Gamma)
, together with the functoriality of the map

p_{\Gamma}:\Tm(\Gamma)\to\Ty(\Gamma)
.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}\mathrel{:}{A}\equiv_{id}{A}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}\quad\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}\mathrel{:}{b}\equiv_{g}{c}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}\circ e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{g\circ f}{c}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}{\Gamma\vdash e\circ\symup{id}\equiv e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}\circ e\equiv e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}\quad\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}\mathrel{:}{b}\equiv_{g}{c}\quad\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{:}{d}\equiv_{h}{c}}{\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime\prime}\circ(e^{\prime}\circ e)\equiv(e^{\prime\prime}\circ e^{\prime})\circ e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{h\circ g\circ f}{d}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}

C.2.
Action of substitutions

The following rules make the assignment
\Gamma\mapsto\Tm(\Gamma)

functorial in
\Gamma
, at the level on morphisms, and ensure that the map

p
is natural.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}\quad\Delta\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{e}[\sigma]\mathrel{:}{\mathord{a}[\sigma]}\equiv_{\mathord{f}[\sigma]}{\mathord{b}[\sigma]}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{e}[\symup{id}]\equiv e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}\quad\Delta\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\quad\Xi\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{e}[\sigma\circ\tau]\equiv\mathord{\mathord{e}[\sigma]}[\tau]\mathrel{:}{\mathord{\mathord{a}[\sigma]}[\tau]}\equiv_{\mathord{\mathord{f}[\sigma]}[\tau]}{\mathord{\mathord{b}[\sigma]}[\tau]}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}

C.3.
Discrete opfibration structure

Finally, we add rules to axiomatize the discrete opfibration structure of the
morphism
p
as follows:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\quad\Gamma\vdash f\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B}{\Gamma\vdash e_{f}({a})\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{a\langle f\rangle}}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}{\Gamma\vdash a\langle f\rangle\equiv b}\frac{\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}{\Gamma\vdash e\equiv e_{f}({a})\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}}

From these rules, it follows that there is at most one witness for the
judgment
{a}\equiv_{f}{b}
up to conversion: if

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}
and

\Gamma\vdash e^{\prime}\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}

then
\Gamma\vdash e\equiv e^{\prime}\mathrel{:}{a}\equiv_{f}{b}
.
In particular, all the categorical equations introduced before are derivable.

This completes the alternate presentation of AdapTT. One can check that
Rule
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
and Rule
2Judgments and rules for AdapTT (excerpt, see )
are
derivable in this presentation. Conversely, the alternate presentation of
AdapTT given here is derivable from the one given in the main text.

C.4.
Functoriality of term constructors

This presentation allows us to rewrite the equality that we require on
term constructors in a different, perhaps more natural or systematic way.
We illustrate this with the example of lists, for which we
have required that adapters behave like generalized map functions as follows

(h\mathbin{:\!:}t)\langle\List(f)\rangle\equiv(h\langle f\rangle)\mathbin{:\!:}(t\langle\List(f)\rangle)

The above constraint can be rephrased in the new formulation as a requirement
for the term constructor to act on morphisms in
\Tm(\Gamma)
, as follows

\frac{\Gamma\vdash e^{h}\mathrel{:}{h}\equiv_{f}{h^{\prime}}\quad\Gamma\vdash e^{t}\mathrel{:}{t}\equiv_{\List(f)}{t^{\prime}}}{\Gamma\vdash e^{h}\mathbin{:\!:}e^{t}\mathrel{:}{h\mathbin{:\!:}t}\equiv_{\List(f)}{h^{\prime}\mathbin{:\!:}t^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}

We did not explore it further, but we believe
there should be an alternate presentation
of AdapTT
2
that follows this style.

Appendix D
Full rules for AdapTT
2

D.1.
All sorts and corresponding judgment forms

\frac{}{\Ctx}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}\Ctx}{\Sub(\Gamma,\Delta)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}\Ctx}\quad\quad\sigma,\tau\mathrel{:}\Sub(\Gamma,\Delta)}{\Trans({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta},\sigma,\tau)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{\Gamma\mathrel{:}\Ctx}{\Ty(\Gamma)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\mathrel{:}\Ctx}\quad\quad A,B\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma)}{\Ad({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},A,B)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\mathrel{:}\Ctx}\quad\quad A\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma)}{\Tm(\Gamma,A)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\\
\vdash\Box\Gamma\vdash\Box\mathrel{:}\Delta\Gamma\vdash\Box\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\tau\Gamma\vdash\Box\Gamma\vdash\Box\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\Gamma\vdash\Box\mathrel{:}A\\
\frac{\Gamma\mathrel{:}\Ctx}{\Tel(\Gamma)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\mathrel{:}\Ctx}\quad\Theta,\Theta^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\Tel(\Gamma)}{\TelAd({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},\Theta,\Theta^{\prime})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\mathrel{:}\Ctx}\quad\Theta\mathrel{:}\Ty(\Gamma)}{\Inst(\Gamma,\Theta)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=,right=}\\
\hskip-20.00003pt\Gamma\vdash\Box\hskip 12.50002pt\Gamma\vdash\Box\mathrel{:}\Theta\Rightarrow\Theta^{\prime}\hskip 30.00005pt\Gamma\vdash\Box\mathrel{:}\Theta\\

D.2.
Basic structure of AdapTT
2

\frac{\vdash\Gamma}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{\Gamma}\mathrel{:}\Gamma}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubId,right=}\frac{\Delta\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\tau\circ\sigma\mathrel{:}\Xi}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubComp,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\circ\symup{id}_{\Gamma}\equiv\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubRightUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{\Delta}\circ\sigma\equiv\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubLeftUnitality,right=}\frac{\Phi\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad\Xi\vdash\delta\mathrel{:}\Phi\quad\Gamma\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\circ(\delta\circ\tau)\equiv(\sigma\circ\delta)\circ\tau\mathrel{:}\Delta}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubAssoc,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash A\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{A}\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow A}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=AdId,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash g\mathrel{:}B\Rightarrow C\quad\Gamma\vdash f\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B}{\Gamma\vdash g\circ f\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow C}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=AdComp,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{\sigma}\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransId,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\rho\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma\quad\Gamma\vdash\nu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\tau\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\nu\circ\mu\mathrel{:}\rho\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\tau}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransComp,right=}\frac{\Delta\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\tau\circ\mu\mathrel{:}(\tau\circ\sigma)\Rightarrow_{\Xi}(\tau\circ\sigma^{\prime})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerLeft,right=}\frac{\Delta\vdash\nu\mathrel{:}\tau\Rightarrow_{\Xi}\tau^{\prime}\quad\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\nu\circ\sigma\mathrel{:}(\tau\circ\sigma)\Rightarrow_{\Xi}(\tau^{\prime}\circ\sigma)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerRight,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\mu\circ\symup{id}_{\sigma}\equiv\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransLeftUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\circ\mu\equiv\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransRightUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\tau\quad\Gamma\vdash\nu\mathrel{:}\tau\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\rho\quad\Gamma\vdash\xi\mathrel{:}\rho\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\psi\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\xi\circ(\nu\circ\mu)\equiv(\xi\circ\nu)\circ\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\psi}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransAssoc,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad\Delta\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\tau\circ\symup{id}_{\sigma}\equiv\symup{id}_{\tau\circ\sigma}\mathrel{:}\tau\circ\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Xi}\tau\circ\sigma}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerLeftRightUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad\Delta\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{\tau}\circ\sigma\equiv\symup{id}_{\tau\circ\sigma}\mathrel{:}\tau\circ\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Xi}\tau\circ\sigma}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerRightLeftUnitality,right=}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{\Delta}\circ\mu\equiv\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerLeftLeftUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\mu\circ\symup{id}_{\Gamma}\equiv\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerRightRightUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\sigma\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\sigma^{\prime}\quad\Delta\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad\Xi\vdash\tau^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\Psi\quad}{\Gamma\vdash(\tau^{\prime}\circ\tau)\circ\mu\equiv\tau^{\prime}\circ(\tau\circ\mu)\mathrel{:}(\tau^{\prime}\circ\tau\circ\sigma)\Rightarrow_{\Psi}(\tau^{\prime}\circ\tau\circ\sigma^{\prime})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerLeftAssoc,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad\Delta\vdash\sigma^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad\Xi\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\tau\Rightarrow_{\Psi}\tau^{\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\mu\circ(\sigma^{\prime}\circ\sigma)\equiv(\mu\circ\sigma^{\prime})\circ\sigma\mathrel{:}(\tau\circ\sigma^{\prime}\circ\sigma)\Rightarrow_{\Psi}(\tau^{\prime}\circ\sigma^{\prime}\circ\sigma^{\prime})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerRightAssoc,right=}\frac{\Delta\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Xi\quad\Gamma\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\tau\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\tau^{\prime}\quad\Gamma\vdash\nu\mathrel{:}\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{\Delta}\tau^{\prime\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\circ(\mu\circ\nu)\equiv(\sigma\circ\mu)\circ(\sigma\circ\nu)\mathrel{:}(\delta\circ\tau)\Rightarrow_{\Xi}(\delta\circ\tau^{\prime\prime})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerLeftDistr,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad\Delta\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}\tau\Rightarrow_{\Xi}\tau^{\prime}\quad\Delta\vdash\nu\mathrel{:}\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{\Xi}\tau^{\prime\prime}\quad}{\Gamma\vdash(\mu\circ\nu)\circ\sigma\equiv(\mu\circ\sigma)\circ(\nu\circ\sigma)\mathrel{:}(\delta\circ\tau)\Rightarrow_{\Xi}(\delta\circ\tau^{\prime\prime})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TransWhiskerRightDistr,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\quad}{\Gamma\vdash a\circ\symup{id}_{A}\equiv a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=AdRightUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\symup{id}_{B}\circ a\equiv a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=AdLeftUnitality,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\quad\Gamma\vdash \par\frac{\Delta\vdash A\quad\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta\quad}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{A}[\sigma]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubTy,right=}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash A}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{A}[\symup{id}_{\Gamma}]\equiv A}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubTyId,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash A\quad\Xi\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\quad\Delta\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi}{\Delta\vdash\mathord{A}[\sigma\circ\tau]\equiv\mathord{\mathord{A}[\sigma]}[\tau]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubTyComp,right=}\par\frac{\Delta\vdash f\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\quad\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{f}[\sigma]\mathrel{:}\mathord{A}[\sigma]\Rightarrow\mathord{B}[\sigma]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubAd,right=}\par\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{a}[\symup{id}_{\Gamma}]\equiv a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubAdId,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\quad\Xi\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Gamma\quad\Delta\vdash\tau\mathrel{:}\Xi}{\Delta\vdash\mathord{a}[\sigma\circ\tau]\equiv\mathord{\mathord{a}[\sigma]}[\tau]\mathrel{:}\mathord{A}[\sigma\circ\tau]\Rightarrow\mathord{B}[\sigma\circ\tau]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubAdComp,right=}\frac{\Delta\vdash A\quad\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{\symup{id}_{A}}[\sigma]\equiv\symup{id}_{\mathord{A}[\sigma]}\mathrel{:}\mathord{A}[\sigma]\Rightarrow\mathord{A}[\sigma]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubAdOnId,right=}\frac{\Delta\vdash a\mathrel{:}A\Rightarrow B\quad\Delta\vdash b\mathrel{:}B\Rightarrow C\quad\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{(b\circ a)}[\sigma]\equiv(\mathord{b}[\sigma])\circ(\mathord{a}[\sigma])\mathrel{:}\mathord{A}[\sigma]\Rightarrow\mathord{C}[\sigma]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubAdOnComp,right=}\par\frac{\Delta\vdash t\mathrel{:}A\quad\Gamma\vdash\sigma\mathrel{:}\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{t}[\sigma]\mathrel{:}\mathord{A}[\sigma]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=SubTm,right=}\\

D.5.
Telescopes

\frac{\vdash\Gamma\quad\Gamma^{d}\vdash\Theta}{\vdash\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}_{d}\Theta}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=CtxExtTel,right=($d:\Dir$)}\frac{\vdash\Gamma}{\Gamma\vdash\mathord{\diamondsuit}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TelEmp,right=}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\quad\Gamma\vdash\Theta\quad\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}_{+}\Theta\vdash A}{\Gamma\vdash\Theta\mathbin{\triangleright}A}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=TelExtTy,right=}\\

D.7.
Rules for Pi

\frac{\Gamma^{-}\vdash A\quad\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}_{-}A\vdash B}{\Gamma\vdash\Pi A\mathchar 58\relax B}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=PiTy,right=}\frac{\Gamma^{-}\vdash D.8.
Inductive types

\par\frac{\vdash\Gamma_{par}\quad\Gamma_{par}\vdash\Theta_{ind}}{\IndDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind})\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\overrightarrow{\ConDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind})}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=DataDescDef,right=}\frac{\vdash\Gamma_{par}\quad\Gamma_{par}\vdash\Theta_{ind}\quad\Gamma_{par}\vdash\Theta_{nr}\quad r\mathrel{:}\overrightarrow{\RecDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind},\Theta_{nr})}\quad\Gamma_{par}\mathbin{\triangleright}\Theta_{nr}\vdash\iota\mathrel{:}\mathord{\Theta_{ind}}[\mathord{\uparrow}]}{\{\symsf{nrec}\coloneq\Theta_{nr};\symsf{rec}\coloneq r;\symsf{ind}\coloneq\iota\}\mathrel{:}\ConDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=ConDescDef,right=}\\
\frac{\vdash\Gamma_{par}\quad\Gamma_{par}\vdash\Theta_{ind}\quad\Gamma_{par}\vdash\Theta_{nr}\quad(\Gamma_{par}\mathbin{\triangleright}\Theta_{nr})^{-}\vdash\Theta_{ar}\quad(\Gamma\mathbin{\triangleright}_{+}\Theta_{nr})\mathbin{\triangleright}_{-}\Theta_{ar}\vdash\iota\mathrel{:}\mathord{\Theta_{ind}}[\mathord{\uparrow}]}{\{\symsf{arit}\coloneq\Theta_{ar};\symsf{rind}\coloneq\iota\}\mathrel{:}\RecDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind},\Theta_{nr})}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=RecDescDef,right=}\par\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\mathrel{:}\IndDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind})}\quad}{\Gamma_{par}\mathbin{\triangleright}\Theta_{ind}\vdash\ind(I)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=IndTy,right=}\frac{I\mathrel{:}\IndDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind})\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c\mathrel{:}\ConDesc({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind}})}\quad 58\relax_{\symsf{rec}})\right)}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=ConDataDef,right=}\frac{\vdash\Gamma_{par}\quad\Gamma_{par}\vdash\Theta_{ind}\quad\Gamma_{par}\vdash\Theta_{nr}\quad 58\relax\mathord{\Ty}_{+})\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathord{\Theta_{nr}}[\mathord{\uparrow}]\vdash\recDatas(\mathord{\diamondsuit})\equiv\mathord{\diamondsuit}}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=RecDatasEmp,right=}\frac{c\mathrel{:}\overrightarrow{\RecDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind},\Theta_{nr})}\quad r\mathrel{:}\RecDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind},\Theta_{nr})\quad}{\Gamma_{par}\mathbin{\blacktriangleright}_{+}(\Theta_{ind}\mathchar 58\relax\mathord{\Ty}_{+})\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathord{\Theta_{nr}}[\mathord{\uparrow}]\vdash\recDatas(c\mathbin{\triangleright}r)\equiv\recDatas(c)\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathord{\recData(r)}[\mathord{\uparrow}]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=RecDatasExt,right=}\frac{r\mathrel{:}\RecDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind},\Theta_{nr})\quad}{\Gamma_{par}\mathbin{\blacktriangleright}_{+}(\Theta_{ind}\mathchar 58\relax\mathord{\Ty}_{+})\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathord{\Theta_{nr}}[\mathord{\uparrow}]\vdash\recData(r)\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\hskip 100.00015pt\\
\hskip 100.00015pt\P\mathord{r\mathchar 58\relax_{\symsf{arit}}}[\mathord{\uparrow}\mathbin{\mathord{\triangleright}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\triangleright}}\symup{id}]\mathchar 58\relax\left(\mathtt{0}^{\symsf{ty}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}[(\mathord{\uparrow}\circ\mathord{\uparrow})\mathbin{\triangleright}(\mathord{r\mathchar 58\relax_{\symsf{rind}}}[(\mathord{\uparrow}\circ\mathord{\uparrow}\circ\mathord{\uparrow})\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathord{\vinst}[\mathord{\uparrow}]\mathbin{\triangleright}\vinst]\right)])}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=RecDataDef,right=}\par\frac{I\mathrel{:}\IndDesc(\Gamma_{par},\mathord{\diamondsuit})\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c\mathrel{:}\ConDesc({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{par},\mathord{\diamondsuit}})}\quad ic\mathrel{:}c\in I\quad\quad\Delta\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}p\Rightarrow_{\Gamma_{par}}p^{\prime}\quad\Delta\vdash arg\mathrel{:}\mathord{\conData}[p\mathbin{\blacktriangleright}\mathord{\ind(I)}[p]]}{\Delta\vdash\mathord{\constr(ic)}[p\mathbin{\triangleright}arg]\langle\mathord{\ind(I)}\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rangle\equiv\hskip 115.00017pt\\
\hskip 30.00005pt\mathord{\constr(ic)}[p^{\prime}\mathbin{\triangleright}arg\langle\mathord{\left(\mathord{\conData(c)}[\symup{id}\mathbin{\blacktriangleright}\ind(I)]\right)}\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rangle]\mathrel{:}\mathord{\ind(I)}[p^{\prime}]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=IndAdEqPar,right=}\frac{I\mathrel{:}\IndDesc(\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind})\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c\mathrel{:}\ConDesc({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{par},\Theta_{ind}})}\quad ic\mathrel{:}c\in I\quad\Delta\vdash\mu\mathrel{:}p\Rightarrow_{\Gamma_{par}}p^{\prime}\quad\Delta\vdash argn\mathrel{:}\mathord{c\mathchar 58\relax_{\symsf{nrec}}}[p]\quad\Delta\vdash argr\mathrel{:}\mathord{\recDatas(c\mathchar 58\relax_{\symsf{rec}})}[p\mathbin{\blacktriangleright}\mathord{\ind(I)}[p\mathbin{\mathord{\triangleright}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\triangleright}}\symup{id}]\mathbin{\triangleright}argn]}{\Delta\vdash\mathord{\constr(ic)}[p\mathbin{\triangleright}argn\mathbin{\triangleright}argr]\langle\mathord{\ind(I)}\llbracket\mu\mathbin{\mathord{\triangleright}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\triangleright}}\mathord{c\mathchar 58\relax_{\symsf{ind}}}[p\mathbin{\triangleright}argn]\rrbracket\rangle\equiv\hskip 30.00005pt\\
\mathord{\constr(ic)}[p^{\prime}\mathbin{\triangleright}(argn\mathbin{\triangleright}argr)\langle\mathord{\left(\mathord{\conData(c)}[\symup{id}\mathbin{\blacktriangleright}\ind(I)]\right)}\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\rangle]\\
\hskip 70.0001pt\mathrel{:}\mathord{\ind(I)}[p^{\prime}\mathbin{\triangleright}\mathord{c\mathchar 58\relax_{\symsf{ind}}}[p\mathbin{\triangleright}argn]\langle{\mathord{\Theta_{ind}}\llbracket\mu\rrbracket}\rangle]}\quad\textsc{vcenter,left=IndAdEq,right=}\par}
